## Principal activities

The C/uni006Fmpany's principal activities during the year ended 30 June 2022 were that /uni006Ff a h/uni006Flding c/uni006Fmpany. Integrated traffic management services include the pr/uni006Fvisi/uni006Fn /uni006Ff traffic c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl, equipment hire, plans and permits, event management and incident resp/uni006Fnse. Traffic management is a legislative requirement with regulati/uni006Fns prescribing the /uni006Fperati/uni006Fnal requirements f/uni006Fr w/uni006Frk /uni006Fn /uni006Fr adjacent t/uni006F r/uni006Fads. Traffic management is an essential service f/uni006Fr civil infrastructure and maintenance w/uni006Frks with c/uni006Fnsistent w/uni006Frkfl/uni006Fw pr/uni006Fvided by investment in new pr/uni006Fjects and recurring maintenance requirements. Traffic management services revenue is earned based /uni006Fn h/uni006Furly /uni006Fr day rates billed f/uni006Fr traffic c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fllers, vehicles and /uni006Fther equipment.

The Gr/uni006Fup has an established and extensive netw/uni006Frk strategically l/uni006Fcated in Queensland and New S/uni006Futh Wales with the ability t/uni006F share res/uni006Furces, service inn/uni006Fvati/uni006Fns and market sect/uni006Fr expertise al/uni006Fng with enhanced efficiencies thr/uni006Fugh ec/uni006Fn/uni006Fmies /uni006Ff scale and pr/uni006Fcurement. ## Independent Auditor's Report to the Members of AVADA Group Limited

## Report on the Audit of the Financial Report

## Opinion

We have audited the financial report of AVADA Group Limited (the 'Company') and its subsidiaries (the 'Group') which comprises the consolidated statement of financial position as at 30 June 2022, the consolidated statement of  profit  or  loss  and  other  comprehensive  income,  the  consolidated  statement  of  changes  in  equity  and  the consolidated statement of cash flows for the year then ended, and notes to the financial statements, including a summary of significant accounting policies and other explanatory information, and the directors' declaration.

In our opinion, the accompanying financial report of the Group is in accordance with the Corporations Act 2001 , including:

1. giving  a  true  and  fair  view  of  the  Group's  financial  position  as  at  30  June  2022  and  of  its  financial performance for the year then ended; and
2. complying with Australian Accounting Standards and the Corporations Regulations 2001 .

## Basis for Opinion

We conducted  our  audit  in  accordance  with  Australian  Auditing  Standards.  Our  responsibilities  under  those standards are further described in the Auditor's Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Report section of our report. We are independent of the Group in accordance with the auditor independence requirements of the Corporations Act 2001 and the ethical requirements of the Accounting Professional &amp; Ethical Standards Board's APES 110 Code of Ethics for Professional Accountants (including Independence Standards) (the Code) that are relevant to our audit of the financial report in Australia. We have also fulfilled our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with the Code.

We confirm that the independence declaration required by the Corporations Act 2001 , which has been given to the directors of the Company, would be in the same terms if given to the directors as at the time of this auditor's report.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a  basis for our opinion.

## Key Audit Matters

Key audit matters are those matters that, in our professional judgement, were of most significance in our audit of the financial report for the current year. These matters were addressed in the context of our audit of the financial report as a whole, and in forming our opinion thereon, and we do not provide a separate opinion on these matters.

Liability limited by a scheme approved under Professional Standards Legislation.

Member of Deloitte Asia Pacific Limited and the Deloitte organisation.

## Independent audit/uni006Fr's rep/uni006Frt (c/uni006Fntinued)

| Key Audit Matter  How  the  scope  of  our  audit  responded  to  the  Key  Audit Matter  Acquisition accounting for the operating entities  As disclosed in Notes 4 and 30, AVADA Group Limited  ('AVADA') acquired the following entities and their  subsidiaries on 15 December 2021:  1.  Verifact Traffic Pty Limited and its  subsidiaries;  2.  A2O Pty Ltd and its subsidiary;  3.  Platinum Traffic Services Pty Ltd;  4.  D&amp;D Services (Australia) Pty Ltd and its  subsidiary; and  5.  The Traffic Marshall Pty Ltd.  The acquisitions constituted business combinations  in accordance with AASB 3  Business Combinations  and AVADA was determined to be the acquirer for  accounting purposes.  In undertaking the acquisition accounting, AVADA is  required to measure the fair value of the  consideration transferred and the fair value of  identifiable assets acquired and liabilities assumed at  the acquisition date with any difference being  recognised as goodwill.  Acquisition accounting requires significant  judgement and complex estimation in respect of:  1.  the determination of the acquirer;  2.  the determination of the consideration paid  or payable, including the contingent  consideration and uplift in value of the  vendor shares; and  3.  the identification and measurement of all  assets and liabilities, including intangible  assets.  The business combination accounting resulted in  goodwill of $38.2m.  In conjunction with our internal specialists, our  procedures included:  1.  Obtaining  an  understanding  of  the  process  flows  and  key  controls  associated  with  the  acquisition accounting.  1.  Assessing the appropriateness of AVADA's  determination of the acquirer.  2.  Evaluating the final purchase price  consideration, including management's  valuation of the contingent consideration  and the vendor shares.  3.  Assessing the intangible assets identified  and the fair values of the acquired assets  and liabilities assumed.  4.  Evaluating the tax effect accounting  because of the business combinations.  We also assessed the adequacy of the financial report  disclosures  setting  out  the  nature  and  basis  of  the  business  combination  accounting  and  the  assumptions  applied  by  management  in  accounting  for the acquisition.   |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

## Independent audit/uni006Fr's rep/uni006Frt (c/uni006Fntinued)

| Key Audit Matter  How  the  scope  of  our  audit  responded  to  the  Key  Audit Matter  Impairments of non-current assets, including goodwill  and other intangible assets.  As disclosed in Notes 4 and 19, at 30 June 2022,  AVADA performed impairment testing on its cash-  generating units ('CGUs'), resulting in impairment  charges of $15m being recognised.  Determining the recoverable amount of the CGUs to  which goodwill is allocated, is a matter of significant  judgement, particularly in the current economic  environment.  We identified four key estimates in management's  determination of the impairment charge:  1.  revenue growth rates;  2.  gross margins;  3.  terminal growth rates; and  4.  discount rates.  In conjunction with our internal specialists our  procedures included:  1.  Obtaining  an  understanding  of  the  process  flows  and  key  controls  associated  with  the  impairment models, approved by the Board,  used to estimate the recoverable amount of  each  CGU  and  impairment  charges,  where  applicable.  2.  Evaluating AVADA's identification of CGUs.  3.  Obtaining an understanding of the  methodology applied by AVADA in  developing its impairment assessments,  including the inherent subjectivity and  complexity of underlying key assumptions.  4.  In conjunction with our valuation specialists,  we have challenged:  1.  the revenue and terminal growth  rates against relevant external data  and historical actuals;  2.  the discount rate by comparing the  rate to our range; and  3.  the gross margins against historical  actuals and recent market trends.  5.  Performing sensitivity analysis and  evaluating management's impairment  assessment for any evidence of  management bias in assumptions and  judgements applied.  We assessed the adequacy of the financial report  disclosures setting out the nature and basis of the  impairment assessment and the assumptions applied  by AVADA in determining the recoverable amount of  the CGUs.   |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

If, based on the work we have performed, we conclude that there is a material misstatement of this other information, we are required to report that fact. We have nothing to report in this regard.

## Independent audit/uni006Fr's rep/uni006Frt (c/uni006Fntinued)

## Responsibilities of the Directors for the Financial Report

The directors of the Company are responsible for the preparation of the financial report that gives a true and fair view in accordance with Australian Accounting Standards and the Corporations Act 2001 and for such internal control as the directors determine is necessary to enable the preparation of the financial report that gives a true and fair view and is free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

In preparing the financial report, the directors are responsible for assessing the ability of the Group to continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern basis of accounting unless the directors either intend to liquidate the Group or to cease operations, or have no realistic alternative but to do so.

## Auditor's Responsibilities for the Audit of the Financial Report

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the financial report as a whole is free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor's report that includes our opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with the Australian Auditing Standards will always detect a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of this financial report.

As part of an audit in accordance with the Australian Auditing Standards, we exercise professional judgement and maintain professional scepticism throughout the audit. We also:

- Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the financial report, whether due to fraud or error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of internal control.
- Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the Group's internal control.
- Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and related disclosures made by the directors.
- Conclude on the appropriateness of the directors' use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on the audit evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast significant doubt on the Group's ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty exists, we are required to draw attention in our auditor's report to the related disclosures in the financial report or, if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor's report. However, future events or conditions may cause the Group to cease to continue as a going concern.
- Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content of the financial report, including the disclosures, and whether the financial report represents the underlying transactions and events in a manner that achieves fair presentation.
- Obtain sufficient appropriate audit evidence regarding the financial information of the entities or business activities within the Group to express an opinion on the financial report. We are responsible for the direction, supervision and performance of the Group's audit. We remain solely responsible for our audit opinion.

We communicate with the directors regarding, among other matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit and significant audit findings, including any significant deficiencies in internal control that we identify during our audit.

## Independent audit/uni006Fr's rep/uni006Frt (c/uni006Fntinued)

We  also  provide  the  directors  with  a  statement  that  we  have  complied  with  relevant  ethical  requirements regarding independence, and to communicate with them all relationships and other matters that may reasonably be thought to bear on our independence, and where applicable, actions taken to eliminate threats or safeguards applied.

From the matters communicated with the directors, we determine those matters that were of most significance in the audit of the financial report of the current period and are therefore the key audit matters. We describe these matters in our auditor's report unless law or regulation precludes public disclosure about the matter or when, in extremely rare circumstances, we determine that a matter should not be communicated in our report because the adverse consequences of doing so would reasonably be expected to outweigh the public interest benefits of such communication.

## (a)  Basis of consolidation

The c/uni006Fns/uni006Flidated financial statements inc/uni006Frp/uni006Frate the financial statements /uni006Ff the C/uni006Fmpany and entities c/uni006Fntr/uni006Flled by the C/uni006Fmpany (its subsidiaries) up t/uni006F 30 June 2022. C/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl is achieved when the C/uni006Fmpany:

- is exp/uni006Fsed, /uni006Fr has rights, t/uni006F variable returns fr/uni006Fm its inv/uni006Flvement with the investee; and
- has the ability t/uni006F use its p/uni006Fwer t/uni006F affect its returns.

The C/uni006Fmpany reassesses whether /uni006Fr n/uni006Ft it c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fls an investee if facts and circumstances indicate that there are changes t/uni006F /uni006Fne /uni006Fr m/uni006Fre /uni006Ff the three elements /uni006Ff c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl listed ab/uni006Fve.

C/uni006Fns/uni006Flidati/uni006Fn /uni006Ff a subsidiary begins when the C/uni006Fmpany /uni006Fbtains c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl /uni006Fver the subsidiary and ceases when the C/uni006Fmpany l/uni006Fses c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl /uni006Ff the subsidiary. ## N/uni006Ftes t/uni006F the c/uni006Fns/uni006Flidated financial statements (c/uni006Fntinued)

G/uni006F/uni006Fdwill is measured as the excess /uni006Ff the sum /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn transferred, the am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff any n/uni006Fn-c/uni006Fntr/uni006Flling interests in the acquiree, and the fair value /uni006Ff the acquirer's previ/uni006Fusly held equity interest in the acquiree (if any) /uni006Fver the net /uni006Ff the acquisiti/uni006Fn-date am/uni006Funts /uni006Ff the identifiable assets acquired and the liabilities assumed. If, after reassessment, the net /uni006Ff the acquisiti/uni006Fn-date am/uni006Funts /uni006Ff the identifiable assets acquired and liabilities assumed exceeds the sum /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn transferred, the am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff any n/uni006Fn-c/uni006Fntr/uni006Flling interests in the acquiree and the fair value /uni006Ff the acquirer's previ/uni006Fusly held interest in the acquiree (if any), the excess is rec/uni006Fgnised immediately in pr/uni006Ffit /uni006Fr l/uni006Fss as a bargain purchase gain.

When the c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn transferred by the Gr/uni006Fup in a business c/uni006Fmbinati/uni006Fn includes a c/uni006Fntingent c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn arrangement, the c/uni006Fntingent c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn is measured at its acquisiti/uni006Fn-date fair value and included as part /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn transferred in a business c/uni006Fmbinati/uni006Fn. Changes in fair value /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fntingent c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn that qualify as measurement peri/uni006Fd adjustments are adjusted retr/uni006Fspectively, with c/uni006Frresp/uni006Fnding adjustments against g/uni006F/uni006Fdwill. Measurement peri/uni006Fd adjustments are adjustments that arise fr/uni006Fm additi/uni006Fnal inf/uni006Frmati/uni006Fn /uni006Fbtained during the 'measurement peri/uni006Fd' (which cann/uni006Ft exceed /uni006Fne year fr/uni006Fm the acquisiti/uni006Fn date) ab/uni006Fut facts and circumstances that existed at the acquisiti/uni006Fn date.

The subsequent acc/uni006Funting f/uni006Fr changes in the fair value /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fntingent c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn that d/uni006F n/uni006Ft qualify as measurement peri/uni006Fd adjustments depends /uni006Fn h/uni006Fw the c/uni006Fntingent c/uni006Fnsiderati/uni006Fn is classified. The measurement /uni006Ff deferred tax liabilities and assets reflects the tax c/uni006Fnsequences that w/uni006Fuld f/uni006Fll/uni006Fw fr/uni006Fm the manner in which the Gr/uni006Fup expects, at the end /uni006Ff the rep/uni006Frting peri/uni006Fd, t/uni006F rec/uni006Fver /uni006Fr settle the carrying am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff its assets and liabilities.

## Current and deferred tax f/uni006Fr the peri/uni006Fd

## (g)  Property, plant and equipment

| Class of fixed asset  Method  Useful life  M/uni006Ft/uni006Fr vehicles  Straight line  8  10 years  Traffic c/uni006Fntr/uni006Fl equipment  Straight line  3  7 years  /uni004Fffice furniture and equipment  Straight line  2  10 years  Right /uni006Ff use assets  Straight line  1  15 years   |

## Derec/uni006Fgniti/uni006Fn /uni006Ff intangible assets

## (i)    Impairment of property, plant and equipment and intangible assets excluding goodwill

At each rep/uni006Frting date, the Gr/uni006Fup reviews the carrying am/uni006Funts /uni006Ff its pr/uni006Fperty, plant and equipment and intangible assets t/uni006F determine whether there is any indicati/uni006Fn that th/uni006Fse assets have suffered an impairment l/uni006Fss. If any such indicati/uni006Fn exists, the rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff the asset is estimated t/uni006F determine the extent /uni006Ff the impairment l/uni006Fss (if any). Where the asset d/uni006Fes n/uni006Ft generate cash fl/uni006Fws that are independent fr/uni006Fm /uni006Fther assets, the Gr/uni006Fup estimates the rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff the cash-generating unit t/uni006F which the asset bel/uni006Fngs.

Rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt is the higher /uni006Ff fair value less c/uni006Fsts /uni006Ff disp/uni006Fsal and value in use. In assessing value in use, the estimated future cash fl/uni006Fws are disc/uni006Funted t/uni006F their present value using a pre-tax disc/uni006Funt rate that reflects current market assessments /uni006Ff the time value /uni006Ff m/uni006Fney and the risks specific t/uni006F the asset f/uni006Fr which the estimates /uni006Ff future cash fl/uni006Fws have n/uni006Ft been adjusted.

If the rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff an asset (/uni006Fr cash-generating unit) is estimated t/uni006F be less than its carrying am/uni006Funt, the carrying am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff the asset (/uni006Fr cash-generating unit) is reduced t/uni006F its rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt. An impairment l/uni006Fss is rec/uni006Fgnised immediately in pr/uni006Ffit /uni006Fr l/uni006Fss, unless the relevant asset is carried at a revalued am/uni006Funt, in which case the impairment l/uni006Fss is treated as a revaluati/uni006Fn decrease and t/uni006F the extent that the impairment l/uni006Fss is greater than the related revaluati/uni006Fn surplus, the excess impairment l/uni006Fss is rec/uni006Fgnised in pr/uni006Ffit /uni006Fr l/uni006Fss.

Where an impairment l/uni006Fss subsequently reverses, the carrying am/uni006Funt /uni006Ff the asset (/uni006Fr cash-generating unit) is increased t/uni006F the revised estimate /uni006Ff its rec/uni006Fverable am/uni006Funt, but s/uni006F that the increased carrying am/uni006Funt d/uni006Fes n/uni006Ft exceed the carrying am/uni006Funt that w/uni006Fuld have been determined had n/uni006F impairment l/uni006Fss been rec/uni006Fgnised f/uni006Fr the asset (/uni006Fr cash-generating unit) in pri/uni006Fr years. A reversal /uni006Ff an impairment l/uni006Fss is rec/uni006Fgnised immediately in pr/uni006Ffit /uni006Fr l/uni006Fss t/uni006F the extent that it eliminates the impairment l/uni006Fss which has been rec/uni006Fgnised f/uni006Fr the asset in pri/uni006Fr years.

When a reas/uni006Fnable and c/uni006Fnsistent basis /uni006Ff all/uni006Fcati/uni006Fn can be identified, c/uni006Frp/uni006Frate assets are als/uni006F all/uni006Fcated t/uni006F individual cash-generating units, /uni006Fr /uni006Ftherwise they are all/uni006Fcated t/uni006F the smallest gr/uni006Fup /uni006Ff cash-generating units f/uni006Fr which a reas/uni006Fnable and c/uni006Fnsistent all/uni006Fcati/uni006Fn basis can be identified.

Intangible assets with an indefinite useful life are tested f/uni006Fr impairment at least annually and whenever there is an indicati/uni006Fn at the end /uni006Ff a rep/uni006Frting peri/uni006Fd that the asset may be impaired.

## (j)  Leases

The Gr/uni006Fup assesses whether a c/uni006Fntract is /uni006Fr c/uni006Fntains a lease, at incepti/uni006Fn /uni006Ff the c/uni006Fntract. The Gr/uni006Fup rec/uni006Fgnises a right-/uni006Ff-use asset and a c/uni006Frresp/uni006Fnding lease liability with respect t/uni006F all lease arrangements in which it is the lessee, except f/uni006Fr sh/uni006Frt-term leases (defined as leases with a lease term /uni006Ff 12 m/uni006Fnths /uni006Fr less) and leases /uni006Ff l/uni006Fw value assets (such as tablets and pers/uni006Fnal c/uni006Fmputers, small items /uni006Ff /uni006Fffice furniture and teleph/uni006Fnes). ## N/uni006Ftes t/uni006F the c/uni006Fns/uni006Flidated financial statements (c/uni006Fntinued)

The lease liability is initially measured at the present value /uni006Ff the lease payments that are n/uni006Ft paid at the c/uni006Fmmencement date, disc/uni006Funted by using the rate implicit in the lease. If this rate cann/uni006Ft be readily determined, the Gr/uni006Fup uses its incremental b/uni006Frr/uni006Fwing rate.

The incremental b/uni006Frr/uni006Fwing rate depends /uni006Fn the term, currency and start date /uni006Ff the lease and is determined based /uni006Fn a series /uni006Ff inputs including: the risk-free rate based /uni006Fn g/uni006Fvernment b/uni006Fnd rates; a c/uni006Funtry-specific risk adjustment; a credit risk adjustment based /uni006Fn b/uni006Fnd yields; and an entity-specific adjustment when the risk pr/uni006Ffile /uni006Ff the entity that enters int/uni006F the lease is different t/uni006F that /uni006Ff the Gr/uni006Fup and the lease d/uni006Fes n/uni006Ft benefit fr/uni006Fm a guarantee fr/uni006Fm the Gr/uni006Fup.

Lease payments included in the measurement /uni006Ff the lease liability c/uni006Fmprise:

- Fixed lease payments (including in-substance fixed payments), less any lease incentives receivable;
- variable lease payments that depend /uni006Fn an index /uni006Fr rate, initially measured using the index /uni006Fr rate at the c/uni006Fmmencement date;
- the am/uni006Funt expected t/uni006F be payable by the lessee under residual value guarantees;
- the exercise price /uni006Ff purchase /uni006Fpti/uni006Fns, if the lessee is reas/uni006Fnably certain t/uni006F exercise the /uni006Fpti/uni006Fns; and
- payments /uni006Ff penalties f/uni006Fr terminating the lease, if the lease term reflects the exercise /uni006Ff an /uni006Fpti/uni006Fn t/uni006F terminate the lease.

The lease liability is presented as a separate line in the c/uni006Fns/uni006Flidated statement /uni006Ff financial p/uni006Fsiti/uni006Fn.

The lease liability is subsequently measured by increasing the carrying am/uni006Funt t/uni006F reflect interest /uni006Fn the lease liability (using the effective interest meth/uni006Fd) and by reducing the carrying am/uni006Funt t/uni006F reflect the lease payments made.

The Gr/uni006Fup remeasures the lease liability (and makes a c/uni006Frresp/uni006Fnding adjustment t/uni006F the related right-/uni006Ff-use asset) whenever:

- The lease term has changed /uni006Fr there is a significant event /uni006Fr change in circumstances resulting in a change in the assessment /uni006Ff exercise /uni006Ff a purchase /uni006Fpti/uni006Fn, in which case the lease liability is remeasured by disc/uni006Funting the revised lease payments using a revised disc/uni006Funt rate.
- The lease payments change due t/uni006F changes in an index /uni006Fr rate /uni006Fr a change in expected payment under a guaranteed residual value, in which cases the lease liability is remeasured by disc/uni006Funting the revised lease payments using an unchanged disc/uni006Funt rate (unless the lease payments change is due t/uni006F a change in a fl/uni006Fating interest rate, in which case a revised disc/uni006Funt rate is used).
- A lease c/uni006Fntract is m/uni006Fdified, and the lease m/uni006Fdificati/uni006Fn is n/uni006Ft acc/uni006Funted f/uni006Fr as a separate lease, in which case the lease liability is remeasured based /uni006Fn the lease term /uni006Ff the m/uni006Fdified lease by disc/uni006Funting the revised lease payments using a revised disc/uni006Funt rate at the effective date /uni006Ff the m/uni006Fdificati/uni006Fn.

The right-/uni006Ff-use assets c/uni006Fmprise the initial measurement /uni006Ff the c/uni006Frresp/uni006Fnding lease liability, lease payments made at /uni006Fr bef/uni006Fre the c/uni006Fmmencement day, less any lease incentives received and any initial direct c/uni006Fsts. They are subsequently measured at c/uni006Fst less accumulated depreciati/uni006Fn and impairment l/uni006Fsses.

Whenever the Gr/uni006Fup incurs an /uni006Fbligati/uni006Fn f/uni006Fr c/uni006Fsts t/uni006F dismantle and rem/uni006Fve a leased asset, rest/uni006Fre the site /uni006Fn which it is l/uni006Fcated /uni006Fr rest/uni006Fre the underlying asset t/uni006F the c/uni006Fnditi/uni006Fn required by the terms and c/uni006Fnditi/uni006Fns /uni006Ff the lease, a pr/uni006Fvisi/uni006Fn is rec/uni006Fgnised and measured under AASB 137 Pr/uni006Fvisi/uni006Fns, C/uni006Fntingent Liabilities and C/uni006Fntingent Assets ('AASB 137'). T/uni006F the extent that the c/uni006Fsts relate t/uni006F a right-/uni006Ff use asset, the c/uni006Fsts are included in the related right-/uni006Ff-use asset, unless th/uni006Fse c/uni006Fsts are incurred t/uni006F pr/uni006Fduce invent/uni006Fries.

Right-/uni006Ff-use assets are depreciated /uni006Fver the sh/uni006Frter peri/uni006Fd /uni006Ff lease term and useful life /uni006Ff the right-/uni006Ff-use asset. If a lease transfers /uni006Fwnership /uni006Ff the underlying asset /uni006Fr the c/uni006Fst /uni006Ff the right-/uni006Ff-use asset reflects that the Gr/uni006Fup expects t/uni006F exercise a purchase /uni006Fpti/uni006Fn, the related right-/uni006Ff-use asset is depreciated /uni006Fver the useful life /uni006Ff the underlying asset. The Gr/uni006Fup applies AASB 136 Impairment /uni006Ff Assets ('AASB 136') t/uni006F determine whether a right-/uni006Ff-use asset is impaired and acc/uni006Funts f/uni006Fr any identified impairment l/uni006Fss as described in the 'Pr/uni006Fperty, Plant and Equipment' p/uni006Flicy.

## N/uni006Ftes t/uni006F the c/uni006Fns/uni006Flidated financial statements (c/uni006Fntinued)

## Make g/uni006F/uni006Fd pr/uni006Fvisi/uni006Fns

## C/uni006Fntingent liabilities acquired in a business c/uni006Fmbinati/uni006Fn

If relevant, this fair value assessment less c/uni006Fsts /uni006Ff disp/uni006Fsal is c/uni006Fnducted by the Direct/uni006Frs based /uni006Fn their extensive kn/uni006Fwledge /uni006Ff the traffic management services industry including the current market c/uni006Fnditi/uni006Fns prevailing in the industry. The value-in-use assessment is c/uni006Fnducted using a disc/uni006Funted cash fl/uni006Fw ('DCF') meth/uni006Fd/uni006Fl/uni006Fgy requiring the Direct/uni006Frs t/uni006F estimate the future cash fl/uni006Fws expected t/uni006F arise fr/uni006Fm the CGUs and then applying a disc/uni006Funt rate t/uni006F calculate the present value.

During the year ended 30 June 2022, all /uni006Ff the Gr/uni006Fup's CGUs were tested f/uni006Fr impairment in acc/uni006Frdance with AASB 136 Impairment /uni006Ff Assets .

The DCF m/uni006Fdels ad/uni006Fpted by the Direct/uni006Frs utilises cash fl/uni006Fw f/uni006Frecasts derived fr/uni006Fm the 2023 financial year budgets appr/uni006Fved by the B/uni006Fard and als/uni006F includes the impact /uni006Ff recent market and envir/uni006Fnmental fact/uni006Frs. After year /uni006Fne, cash fl/uni006Fws are increased by a range /uni006Ff gr/uni006Fwth rates t/uni006F year five that d/uni006F n/uni006Ft exceed the relevant industry f/uni006Frecast. ## 35.  Financial risk management

The Gr/uni006Fup's activities exp/uni006Fse it t/uni006F market risk (including interest rate risk and price risk), credit risk and liquidity risk. The Gr/uni006Fup's /uni006Fverall risk management strategy seeks t/uni006F minimise any adverse effects fr/uni006Fm the unpredictability /uni006Ff financial markets /uni006Fn the Gr/uni006Fup's financial perf/uni006Frmance.

## Interest rate risk

The Gr/uni006Fup's main exp/uni006Fsure t/uni006F interest rate risk is l/uni006Fng-term b/uni006Frr/uni006Fwings. Interest rate risk is the risk that the fair value /uni006Fr future cash fl/uni006Fws /uni006Ff a financial asset /uni006Fr financial liability will change as a result /uni006Ff changes in market interest rates. The Gr/uni006Fup is exp/uni006Fsed t/uni006F interest rate risk thr/uni006Fugh its Banking Facilities, which include fl/uni006Fating rate facilities. The Gr/uni006Fup is exp/uni006Fsed t/uni006F interest rate risk because entities in the Gr/uni006Fup b/uni006Frr/uni006Fw funds at b/uni006Fth fixed and fl/uni006Fating interest rates.

## Interest rate risk management

The risk is managed by the Gr/uni006Fup by maintaining an appr/uni006Fpriate mix between fixed and fl/uni006Fating rate b/uni006Frr/uni006Fwings.

The Gr/uni006Fup's exp/uni006Fsures t/uni006F interest rates /uni006Fn financial assets and financial liabilities are detailed in the liquidity risk management secti/uni006Fn /uni006Ff this n/uni006Fte. The Gr/uni006Fup is exp/uni006Fsed t/uni006F BBSY in respect /uni006Ff interest rate benchmarks f/uni006Fr b/uni006Frr/uni006Fwings. The Gr/uni006Fup d/uni006Fes n/uni006Ft currently have any hedging activities /uni006Fr interest rate swaps implemented.
